The game will be simple, with a clean interface, and will allow players to restart the game easily.","File list":["app.py","game_logic.py","templates/index.html","static/css/style.css","static/js/script.js"],"Data structures and interfaces":"\nclassDiagram\n    class GameLogic {\n        -board: list\n        -current_player: str\n        -ai_player: str\n        +__init__(ai_difficulty: str)\n        +make_move(position: int) bool\n        +check_winner() str\n        +is_board_full() bool\n        +reset_board()\n        +get_board() list\n        +ai_move() int\n    }\n    class FlaskApp {\n        -game: GameLogic\n        +__init__()\n        +index() str\n        +make_move(position: int) str\n        +reset_game() str\n    }\n    FlaskApp --> GameLogic\n","Program call flow":"\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant U as User\n    participant F as FlaskApp\n    participant G as GameLogic\n    U->>F: Access game (GET /)\n    F->>G: Initialize game\n    G-->>F: Return initial board state\n    F-->>U: Render game UI\n    U->>F: Make move (POST /move)\n    F->>G: make_move(position)\n    G-->>F: Return updated board state\n    F->>G: check_winner()\n    G-->>F: Return winner or None\n    F-->>U: Render updated UI\n    U->>F: Reset game (POST /reset)\n    F->>G: reset_board()\n    G-->>F: Return reset board state\n    F-->>U: Render reset UI\n","Anything UNCLEAR":"The specific difficulty levels for the AI opponent need further clarification. The `ui.py` file handles all graphical rendering and user input, while `ai.py` manages the AI\'s decision-making process.","Anything UNCLEAR":"The specific AI difficulty levels for single-player mode need further clarification. For now, we will implement a basic AI using the minimax algorithm, but additional difficulty levels (e.g., easy, medium, hard) could be added later."}\n\n## Legacy Code\n```Code\n----- game.py\n```## game.py\nimport pygame\nfrom typing import List, Tuple, Optional\n\nclass Game:\n    def __init__(self):\n        """Initialize the game with an empty board, starting player as \'X\', and game_over as False."""\n        self.board: List[List[Optional[str]]] = [[None for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]\n        self.current_player: str = \'X\'\n        self.game_over: bool = False\n\n    def make_move(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:\n        """\n        Make a move on the board if the cell is empty.\n\n        Args:\n            row (int): The row index of the cell.\n            col (int): The column index of the cell.\n\n        Returns:\n            bool: True if the move was successful, False otherwise.\n        """\n        if self.board[row][col] is None and not self.game_over:\n            self.board[row][col] = self.current_player\n            self.current_player = \'O\' if self.current_player == \'X\' else \'X\'\n            return True\n        return False\n\n    def check_win(self) -> Optional[str]:\n        """\n        Check if the current board state has a winner.\n\n        Returns:\n            Optional[str]: The winning player (\'X\' or \'O\') if there is a winner, None otherwise.\n        """\n        # Check rows\n        for row in self.board:\n            if row[0] == row[1] == row[2] and row[0] is not None:\n                return row[0]\n\n        # Check columns\n        for col in range(3):\n            if self.board[0][col] == self.board[1][col] == self.board[2][col] and self.board[0][col] is not None:\n                return self.board[0][col]\n\n        # Check diagonals\n        if self.board[0][0] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][2] and self.board[0][0] is not None:\n            return self.board[0][0]\n        if self.board[0][2] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][0] and self.board[0][2] is not None:\n            return self.board[0][2]\n\n        # Check for a draw\n        if all(cell is not None for row in self.board for cell in row):\n            return \'Draw\'\n\n        return None\n\n    def reset_game(self):\n        """Reset the game to its initial state."""\n        self.board = [[None for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]\n        self.current_player = \'X\'\n        self.game_over = False\n\n    def get_current_player(self) -> str:\n        """\n        Get the current player.\n\n        Returns:\n            str: The current player (\'X\' or \'O\').\n        """\n        return self.current_player\n\n    def is_game_over(self) -> bool:\n        """\n        Check if the game is over.\n\n        Returns:\n            bool: True if the game is over, False otherwise.\n        """\n        return self.game_over\n\n    def set_game_over(self, game_over: bool):\n        """\n        Set the game over state.\n\n        Args:\n            game_over (bool): The new game over state.\n        """\n        self.game_over = game_over\n```\n```\n\n## Debug logs\n```text\n\n\n\n```\n\n## Bug Feedback logs\n```text\n\n```\n\n# Format example\n## Code: ai.py\n```python\n## ai.py\n...\n```\n\n# Instruction: Based on the context, follow "Format example", write code.\n\n## Code: ai.py. game.py\nimport pygame\nfrom typing import List, Tuple, Optional\n\nclass Game:\n    def __init__(self):\n        """Initialize the game with an empty board, starting player as \'X\', and game_over as False."""\n        self.board: List[List[Optional[str]]] = [[None for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]\n        self.current_player: str = \'X\'\n        self.game_over: bool = False\n\n    def make_move(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:\n        """\n        Make a move on the board if the cell is empty.\n\n        Args:\n            row (int): The row index of the cell.\n            col (int): The column index of the cell.\n\n        Returns:\n            bool: True if the move was successful, False otherwise.\n        """\n        if self.board[row][col] is None and not self.game_over:\n            self.board[row][col] = self.current_player\n            self.current_player = \'O\' if self.current_player == \'X\' else \'X\'\n            return True\n        return False\n\n    def check_win(self) -> Optional[str]:\n        """\n        Check if the current board state has a winner.\n\n        Returns:\n            Optional[str]: The winning player (\'X\' or \'O\') if there is a winner, None otherwise.\n        """\n        # Check rows\n        for row in self.board:\n            if row[0] == row[1] == row[2] and row[0] is not None:\n                return row[0]\n\n        # Check columns\n        for col in range(3):\n            if self.board[0][col] == self.board[1][col] == self.board[2][col] and self.board[0][col] is not None:\n                return self.board[0][col]\n\n        # Check diagonals\n        if self.board[0][0] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][2] and self.board[0][0] is not None:\n            return self.board[0][0]\n        if self.board[0][2] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][0] and self.board[0][2] is not None:\n            return self.board[0][2]\n\n        # Check for a draw\n        if all(cell is not None for row in self.board for cell in row):\n            return \'Draw\'\n\n        return None\n\n    def reset_game(self):\n        """Reset the game to its initial state."""\n        self.board = [[None for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]\n        self.current_player = \'X\'\n        self.game_over = False\n\n    def get_current_player(self) -> str:\n        """\n        Get the current player.\n\n        Returns:\n            str: The current player (\'X\' or \'O\').\n        """\n        return self.current_player\n\n    def is_game_over(self) -> bool:\n        """\n        Check if the game is over.\n\n        Returns:\n            bool: True if the game is over, False otherwise.\n        """\n        return self.game_over\n\n    def set_game_over(self, game_over: bool):\n        """\n        Set the game over state.\n\n        Args:\n            game_over (bool): The new game over state.\n        """\n        self.game_over = game_over\n```\n----- ai.py\n```## ai.py\nfrom typing import List, Optional, Tuple\n\nclass AI:\n    def __init__(self, difficulty: str = \'medium\'):\n        """\n        Initialize the AI with a specified difficulty level.\n\n        Args:\n            difficulty (str): The difficulty level of the AI (\'easy\', \'medium\', \'hard\').\n                             Defaults to \'medium\'.\n        """\n        self.difficulty = difficulty\n\n    def get_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Determine the AI\'s move based on the current board state.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        if self.difficulty == \'easy\':\n            return self._get_random_move(board)\n        elif self.difficulty == \'medium\':\n            return self._get_medium_move(board)\n        elif self.difficulty == \'hard\':\n            return self._get_minimax_move(board)\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(f"Unknown difficulty level: {self.difficulty}")\n\n    def _get_random_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Get a random valid move from the available empty cells.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of a random valid move.\n        """\n        import random\n        empty_cells = [(row, col) for row in range(3) for col in range(3) if board[row][col] is None]\n        return random.choice(empty_cells)\n\n    def _get_medium_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Get a move that is slightly smarter than random, but not as complex as minimax.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        # First, check if the AI can win in the next move\n        for row in range(3):\n            for col in range(3):\n                if board[row][col] is None:\n                    board[row][col] = \'O\'\n                    if self._check_win(board) == \'O\':\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        return (row, col)\n                    board[row][col] = None\n\n        # Then, check if the player can win in the next move and block them\n        for row in range(3):\n            for col in range(3):\n                if board[row][col] is None:\n                    board[row][col] = \'X\'\n                    if self._check_win(board) == \'X\':\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        return (row, col)\n                    board[row][col] = None\n\n        # If no immediate win or block, return a random move\n        return self._get_random_move(board)\n\n    def _get_minimax_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Get the best move using the minimax algorithm.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        best_score = -float(\'inf\')\n        best_move = None\n\n        for row in range(3):\n            for col in range(3):\n                if board[row][col] is None:\n                    board[row][col] = \'O\'\n                    score = self._minimax(board, 0, False)\n                    board[row][col] = None\n                    if score > best_score:\n                        best_score = score\n                        best_move = (row, col)\n\n        return best_move\n\n    def _minimax(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]], depth: int, is_maximizing: bool) -> int:\n        """\n        Recursive minimax algorithm to determine the best move.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n            depth (int): The current depth in the recursion tree.\n            is_maximizing (bool): Whether the current player is maximizing or minimizing.\n\n        Returns:\n            int: The score of the current board state.\n        """\n        result = self._check_win(board)\n        if result is not None:\n            if result == \'O\':\n                return 10 - depth\n            elif result == \'X\':\n                return depth - 10\n            else:\n                return 0\n\n        if is_maximizing:\n            best_score = -float(\'inf\')\n            for row in range(3):\n                for col in range(3):\n                    if board[row][col] is None:\n                        board[row][col] = \'O\'\n                        score = self._minimax(board, depth + 1, False)\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        best_score = max(score, best_score)\n            return best_score\n        else:\n            best_score = float(\'inf\')\n            for row in range(3):\n                for col in range(3):\n                    if board[row][col] is None:\n                        board[row][col] = \'X\'\n                        score = self._minimax(board, depth + 1, True)\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        best_score = min(score, best_score)\n            return best_score\n\n    def _check_win(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Optional[str]:\n        """\n        Check if the current board state has a winner.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Optional[str]: The winning player (\'X\' or \'O\') if there is a winner, None otherwise.\n        """\n        # Check rows\n        for row in board:\n            if row[0] == row[1] == row[2] and row[0] is not None:\n                return row[0]\n\n        # Check columns\n        for col in range(3):\n            if board[0][col] == board[1][col] == board[2][col] and board[0][col] is not None:\n                return board[0][col]\n\n        # Check diagonals\n        if board[0][0] == board[1][1] == board[2][2] and board[0][0] is not None:\n            return board[0][0]\n        if board[0][2] == board[1][1] == board[2][0] and board[0][2] is not None:\n            return board[0][2]\n\n        # Check for a draw\n        if all(cell is not None for row in board for cell in row):\n            return \'Draw\'\n\n        return None\n```\n```\n\n## Debug logs\n```text\n\n\n\n```\n\n## Bug Feedback logs\n```text\n\n```\n\n# Format example\n## Code: ui.py\n```python\n## ui.py\n...\n```\n\n# game.py\nimport pygame\nfrom typing import List, Tuple, Optional\n\nclass Game:\n    def __init__(self):\n        """Initialize the game with an empty board, starting player as \'X\', and game_over as False."""\n        self.board: List[List[Optional[str]]] = [[None for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]\n        self.current_player: str = \'X\'\n        self.game_over: bool = False\n\n    def make_move(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:\n        """\n        Make a move on the board if the cell is empty.\n\n        Args:\n            row (int): The row index of the cell.\n            col (int): The column index of the cell.\n\n        Returns:\n            bool: True if the move was successful, False otherwise.\n        """\n        if self.board[row][col] is None and not self.game_over:\n            self.board[row][col] = self.current_player\n            self.current_player = \'O\' if self.current_player == \'X\' else \'X\'\n            return True\n        return False\n\n    def check_win(self) -> Optional[str]:\n        """\n        Check if the current board state has a winner.\n\n        Returns:\n            Optional[str]: The winning player (\'X\' or \'O\') if there is a winner, None otherwise.\n        """\n        # Check rows\n        for row in self.board:\n            if row[0] == row[1] == row[2] and row[0] is not None:\n                return row[0]\n\n        # Check columns\n        for col in range(3):\n            if self.board[0][col] == self.board[1][col] == self.board[2][col] and self.board[0][col] is not None:\n                return self.board[0][col]\n\n        # Check diagonals\n        if self.board[0][0] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][2] and self.board[0][0] is not None:\n            return self.board[0][0]\n        if self.board[0][2] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][0] and self.board[0][2] is not None:\n            return self.board[0][2]\n\n        # Check for a draw\n        if all(cell is not None for row in self.board for cell in row):\n            return \'Draw\'\n\n        return None\n\n    def reset_game(self):\n        """Reset the game to its initial state."""\n        self.board = [[None for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]\n        self.current_player = \'X\'\n        self.game_over = False\n\n    def get_current_player(self) -> str:\n        """\n        Get the current player.\n\n        Returns:\n            str: The current player (\'X\' or \'O\').\n        """\n        return self.current_player\n\n    def is_game_over(self) -> bool:\n        """\n        Check if the game is over.\n\n        Returns:\n            bool: True if the game is over, False otherwise.\n        """\n        return self.game_over\n\n    def set_game_over(self, game_over: bool):\n        """\n        Set the game over state.\n\n        Args:\n            game_over (bool): The new game over state.\n        """\n        self.game_over = game_over\n```\n----- ai.py\n```## ai.py\nfrom typing import List, Optional, Tuple\n\nclass AI:\n    def __init__(self, difficulty: str = \'medium\'):\n        """\n        Initialize the AI with a specified difficulty level.\n\n        Args:\n            difficulty (str): The difficulty level of the AI (\'easy\', \'medium\', \'hard\').\n                             Defaults to \'medium\'.\n        """\n        self.difficulty = difficulty\n\n    def get_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Determine the AI\'s move based on the current board state.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        if self.difficulty == \'easy\':\n            return self._get_random_move(board)\n        elif self.difficulty == \'medium\':\n            return self._get_medium_move(board)\n        elif self.difficulty == \'hard\':\n            return self._get_minimax_move(board)\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(f"Unknown difficulty level: {self.difficulty}")\n\n    def _get_random_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Get a random valid move from the available empty cells.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of a random valid move.\n        """\n        import random\n        empty_cells = [(row, col) for row in range(3) for col in range(3) if board[row][col] is None]\n        return random.choice(empty_cells)\n\n    def _get_medium_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Get a move that is slightly smarter than random, but not as complex as minimax.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        # First, check if the AI can win in the next move\n        for row in range(3):\n            for col in range(3):\n                if board[row][col] is None:\n                    board[row][col] = \'O\'\n                    if self._check_win(board) == \'O\':\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        return (row, col)\n                    board[row][col] = None\n\n        # Then, check if the player can win in the next move and block them\n        for row in range(3):\n            for col in range(3):\n                if board[row][col] is None:\n                    board[row][col] = \'X\'\n                    if self._check_win(board) == \'X\':\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        return (row, col)\n                    board[row][col] = None\n\n        # If no immediate win or block, return a random move\n        return self._get_random_move(board)\n\n    def _get_minimax_move(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Tuple[int, int]:\n        """\n        Get the best move using the minimax algorithm.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        best_score = -float(\'inf\')\n        best_move = None\n\n        for row in range(3):\n            for col in range(3):\n                if board[row][col] is None:\n                    board[row][col] = \'O\'\n                    score = self._minimax(board, 0, False)\n                    board[row][col] = None\n                    if score > best_score:\n                        best_score = score\n                        best_move = (row, col)\n\n        return best_move\n\n    def _minimax(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]], depth: int, is_maximizing: bool) -> int:\n        """\n        Recursive minimax algorithm to determine the best move.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n            depth (int): The current depth in the recursion tree.\n            is_maximizing (bool): Whether the current player is maximizing or minimizing.\n\n        Returns:\n            int: The score of the current board state.\n        """\n        result = self._check_win(board)\n        if result is not None:\n            if result == \'O\':\n                return 10 - depth\n            elif result == \'X\':\n                return depth - 10\n            else:\n                return 0\n\n        if is_maximizing:\n            best_score = -float(\'inf\')\n            for row in range(3):\n                for col in range(3):\n                    if board[row][col] is None:\n                        board[row][col] = \'O\'\n                        score = self._minimax(board, depth + 1, False)\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        best_score = max(score, best_score)\n            return best_score\n        else:\n            best_score = float(\'inf\')\n            for row in range(3):\n                for col in range(3):\n                    if board[row][col] is None:\n                        board[row][col] = \'X\'\n                        score = self._minimax(board, depth + 1, True)\n                        board[row][col] = None\n                        best_score = min(score, best_score)\n            return best_score\n\n    def _check_win(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]) -> Optional[str]:\n        """\n        Check if the current board state has a winner.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Optional[str]: The winning player (\'X\' or \'O\') if there is a winner, None otherwise.\n        """\n        # Check rows\n        for row in board:\n            if row[0] == row[1] == row[2] and row[0] is not None:\n                return row[0]\n\n        # Check columns\n        for col in range(3):\n            if board[0][col] == board[1][col] == board[2][col] and board[0][col] is not None:\n                return board[0][col]\n\n        # Check diagonals\n        if board[0][0] == board[1][1] == board[2][2] and board[0][0] is not None:\n            return board[0][0]\n        if board[0][2] == board[1][1] == board[2][0] and board[0][2] is not None:\n            return board[0][2]\n\n        # Check for a draw\n        if all(cell is not None for row in board for cell in row):\n            return \'Draw\'\n\n        return None\n```\n----- ui.py\n```## ui.py\nimport pygame\nfrom typing import List, Optional, Tuple\n\nclass UI:\n    def __init__(self):\n        """\n        Initialize the UI with a Pygame window and font for rendering text.\n        """\n        pygame.init()\n        self.screen = pygame.display.set_mode((300, 300))\n        pygame.display.set_caption("Tic Tac Toe")\n        self.font = pygame.font.Font(None, 36)\n        self.cell_size = 100\n\n    def draw_board(self, board: List[List[Optional[str]]]):\n        """\n        Draw the Tic Tac Toe board on the screen.\n\n        Args:\n            board (List[List[Optional[str]]]): The current state of the game board.\n        """\n        self.screen.fill((255, 255, 255))  # Clear screen with white background\n\n        # Draw grid lines\n        for i in range(1, 3):\n            pygame.draw.line(self.screen, (0, 0, 0), (i * self.cell_size, 0), (i * self.cell_size, 300), 2)\n            pygame.draw.line(self.screen, (0, 0, 0), (0, i * self.cell_size), (300, i * self.cell_size), 2)\n\n        # Draw X and O\n        for row in range(3):\n            for col in range(3):\n                if board[row][col] == \'X\':\n                    self._draw_x(row, col)\n                elif board[row][col] == \'O\':\n                    self._draw_o(row, col)\n\n        pygame.display.flip()\n\n    def _draw_x(self, row: int, col: int):\n        """\n        Draw an \'X\' in the specified cell.\n\n        Args:\n            row (int): The row index of the cell.\n            col (int): The column index of the cell.\n        """\n        x = col * self.cell_size\n        y = row * self.cell_size\n        pygame.draw.line(self.screen, (255, 0, 0), (x + 10, y + 10), (x + 90, y + 90), 2)\n        pygame.draw.line(self.screen, (255, 0, 0), (x + 90, y + 10), (x + 10, y + 90), 2)\n\n    def _draw_o(self, row: int, col: int):\n        """\n        Draw an \'O\' in the specified cell.\n\n        Args:\n            row (int): The row index of the cell.\n            col (int): The column index of the cell.\n        """\n        x = col * self.cell_size + 50\n        y = row * self.cell_size + 50\n        pygame.draw.circle(self.screen, (0, 0, 255), (x, y), 40, 2)\n\n    def display_message(self, message: str):\n        """\n        Display a message on the screen.\n\n        Args:\n            message (str): The message to display.\n        """\n        text = self.font.render(message, True, (0, 0, 0))\n        text_rect = text.get_rect(center=(150, 150))\n        self.screen.fill((255, 255, 255))\n        self.screen.blit(text, text_rect)\n        pygame.display.flip()\n\n    def get_click_position(self) -> Optional[Tuple[int, int]]:\n        """\n        Get the position of a mouse click on the board.\n\n        Returns:\n            Optional[Tuple[int, int]]: The row and column indices of the clicked cell, or None if no valid click.\n        """\n        while True:\n            for event in pygame.event.get():\n                if event.type == pygame.QUIT:\n                    pygame.quit()\n                    return None\n                if event.type == pygame.MOUSEBUTTONDOWN:\n                    x, y = pygame.mouse.get_pos()\n                    row = y // self.cell_size\n                    col = x // self.cell_size\n                    return (row, col)\n```\n```\n\n## Debug logs\n```text\n\n\n\n```\n\n## Bug Feedback logs\n```text\n\n```\n\n# Format example\n## Code: main.py\n```python\n## main.py\n...\n```\n\n# ----- game.py
```## game.py

class Game:
    """Represents the Tic Tac Toe game logic."""

    def __init__(self):
        """Initializes the game with an empty board and sets the starting player."""
        self.board = [[' ' for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]
        self.current_player = 'X'  # X always starts first
        self.winner = None

    def make_move(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:
        """Attempts to make a move on the board.

        Args:
            row (int): The row index of the move.
            col (int): The column index of the move.

        Returns:
            bool: True if the move was successful, False otherwise.
        """
        if self.board[row][col] == ' ' and not self.winner:
            self.board[row][col] = self.current_player
            self.winner = self.check_winner()
            if not self.winner:
                self.current_player = 'O' if self.current_player == 'X' else 'X'
            return True
        return False

    def check_winner(self) -> str:
        """Checks if there is a winner on the current board.

        Returns:
            str: The winning player ('X' or 'O'), or None if there is no winner.
        """
        # Check rows
        for row in self.board:
            if row[0] == row[1] == row[2] != ' ':
                return row[0]

        # Check columns
        for col in range(3):
            if self.board[0][col] == self.board[1][col] == self.board[2][col] != ' ':
                return self.board[0][col]

        # Check diagonals
        if self.board[0][0] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][2] != ' ':
            return self.board[0][0]
        if self.board[0][2] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][0] != ' ':
            return self.board[0][2]

        return None

    def is_board_full(self) -> bool:
        """Checks if the board is full.

        Returns:
            bool: True if the board is full, False otherwise.
        """
        for row in self.board:
            if ' ' in row:
                return False
        return True

    def reset_game(self):
        """Resets the game to its initial state."""
        self.board = [[' ' for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]
        self.current_player = 'X'
        self.winner = None
```

----- game.py
```## game.py

class Game:
    """Represents the Tic Tac Toe game logic."""

    def __init__(self):
        """Initializes the game with an empty board and sets the starting player."""
        self.board = [[' ' for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]
        self.current_player = 'X'  # X always starts first
        self.winner = None

    def make_move(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:
        """Attempts to make a move on the board.

        Args:
            row (int): The row index of the move.
            col (int): The column index of the move.

        Returns:
            bool: True if the move was successful, False otherwise.
        """
        if self.board[row][col] == ' ' and not self.winner:
            self.board[row][col] = self.current_player
            self.winner = self.check_winner()
            if not self.winner:
                self.current_player = 'O' if self.current_player == 'X' else 'X'
            return True
        return False

    def check_winner(self) -> str:
        """Checks if there is a winner on the current board.

        Returns:
            str: The winning player ('X' or 'O'), or None if there is no winner.
        """
        # Check rows
        for row in self.board:
            if row[0] == row[1] == row[2] != ' ':
                return row[0]

        # Check columns
        for col in range(3):
            if self.board[0][col] == self.board[1][col] == self.board[2][col] != ' ':
                return self.board[0][col]

        # Check diagonals
        if self.board[0][0] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][2] != ' ':
            return self.board[0][0]
        if self.board[0][2] == self.board[1][1] == self.board[2][0] != ' ':
            return self.board[0][2]

        return None

    def is_board_full(self) -> bool:
        """Checks if the board is full.

        Returns:
            bool: True if the board is full, False otherwise.
        """
        for row in self.board:
            if ' ' in row:
                return False
        return True

    def reset_game(self):
        """Resets the game to its initial state."""
        self.board = [[' ' for _ in range(3)] for _ in range(3)]
        self.current_player = 'X'
        self.winner = None
```
----- ai.py
```## ai.py

class AI:
    """Represents the AI player in the Tic Tac Toe game."""

    def __init__(self, difficulty: str = "easy"):
        """Initializes the AI with a specified difficulty level.

        Args:
            difficulty (str): The difficulty level of the AI. Defaults to "easy".
        """
        self.difficulty = difficulty

    def make_move(self, board: list) -> tuple[int, int]:
        """Determines the AI's move based on the current board state.

        Args:
            board (list): The current state of the game board.

        Returns:
            tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI's move.
        """
        if self.difficulty == "easy":
            return self._make_random_move(board)
        # Add more difficulty levels here if needed
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown difficulty level: {self.difficulty}")

    def _make_random_move(self, board: list) -> tuple[int, int]:
        """Makes a random move on the board.

        Args:
            board (list): The current state of the game board.

        Returns:
            tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI's move.
        """
        import random
        empty_cells = [(row, col) for row in range(3) for col in range(3) if board[row][col] == ' ']
        return random.choice(empty_cells) if empty_cells else (0, 0)

Defaults to "easy".\n        """\n        self.difficulty = difficulty\n\n    def make_move(self, board: list) -> tuple[int, int]:\n        """Determines the AI\'s move based on the current board state.\n\n        Args:\n            board (list): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        if self.difficulty == "easy":\n            return self._make_random_move(board)\n        # Add more difficulty levels here if needed\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(f"Unknown difficulty level: {self.difficulty}")\n\n    def _make_random_move(self, board: list) -> tuple[int, int]:\n        """Makes a random move on the board.\n\n        Args:\n            board (list): The current state of the game board.\n\n        Returns:\n            tuple[int, int]: The row and column indices of the AI\'s move.\n        """\n        import random\n        empty_cells = [(row, col) for row in range(3) for col in range(3) if board[row][col] == \' \']\n        return random.choice(empty_cells) if empty_cells else (0, 0)\n```\n```\n\n## Debug logs\n```text\n\n\n\n```\n\n## Bug Feedback logs\n```text\n\n```\n\n# Format example\n## Code: ui.py\n```python\n## ui.py\n...\n```\n\n# Instruction: Based on the context, follow "Format example", write code.\n\n## Code: ui.py.
